In a project built from this framework most of those are absent; the report below is then the whole of your discovery, and a document you cannot open is never a document you may summarise.\n\n**Start here — the command from *Orient yourself first*, re-read now with the goal in mind:**\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- project doctor --json # FIRST: is this project even coherent? (composition,\n                                     # module state, stale plans, skill drift, broken links,\n                                     # tracked artifacts). ~150 ms. Read `problems` and stop\n                                     # if the composition is invalid — everything after it is\n                                     # guesswork against a project that does not load.\nnpm run crm -- app inspect --json    # installed packages, the resolved capability\n                                     # graph, records and their revisions, actions,\n                                     # policies, providers, problems, limitations\n```\n\nRead it in this order, and do not skip a step:\n\n1. `valid` — is the composition sound at all?\n2. `problems[]` — every missing capability, version mismatch, collision and cycle, each with a code. Fix or report these before planning anything on top of them.\n3. `limitations[]` — what the report **cannot** know. Each has a machine-readable code; treat every one as a hard boundary on what you may claim.\n4. `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]` — what you may build on.\n\nExit codes are the contract: `0` valid · `1` problems (the full report is still printed) · `2` the project could not be read.\n\nThen, where you need per-package or runtime detail:\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- package inspect <package-dir>    # one package in isolation\n```\n\n```text\nGET /api/schema     # the same picture from a running server\n```\n\nGuide, where the project carries it: `docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`. The report's own `limitations[]` state the same boundaries without it.\n\n**What `app inspect` does not tell you, and you must not infer:**\n\n- whether any **database** has applied a migration, holds data, or holds *good* data — it reads source only;\n- whether a **provider** is configured, authenticated or reachable. A provider entry means a definition was composed in source, nothing more. Never infer credentials;\n- any **runtime authorization**. There is no auth, tenancy or RBAC in this framework, so no role is enforced anywhere;\n- **JTBD or quality-gate status.** `evidence.status` is `not_aggregated` and carries paths, not claims. Read the documents yourself.\n\nVerify the reliability of source **data** separately: `app inspect` says a record exists, never that its rows are complete, deduplicated or correct. Count the nulls yourself.\n\nThe JTBD matrix is the repository's own honest statement of what is supported. Treat a row marked `not supported` as authoritative over your intuition.\n\n**Where the report will look thinner than the repository.** Three domains — Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, and Signature & Order — predate the domain package seam and live in `packages/core/src/`. Their actions and record revisions are reported; there is **no package or capability of theirs to cite**, and that absence means \"built before the seam\", not \"missing\". `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` records exactly what each domain has. Do not plan an extraction to fix it: extraction is sequenced work with its own gate, and a goal-driven build is not the place for it.\n\n**How you reach these surfaces.** A shell command, an exit code and JSON — that is the whole requirement (`docs/AGENT_HARNESS_COMPATIBILITY.md`). The MCP server this repository ships (`docs/MCP.md`) predates AX1 and AX2 and exposes a sample domain rather than these contracts; prefer `app inspect`. Mirroring the stable contracts as MCP reads is **DX13, not built**, and `docs/architecture/AGENT_TOOL_SURFACE.md` is the policy it would be built against — read it before proposing any new tool, and never claim a tool listed there exists.\n\n## 3. Assess the gap in writing\n\nProduce: capability coverage, package reuse, missing capabilities, **data-quality gaps**, provider gaps, hard dependencies, approval requirements and risks. Count the nulls before quoting a rate — a metric computed over mostly-missing data is misleading, and saying so is your job.\n\n## 4. Design: reuse, then extend, never patch the kernel\n\n1. **Prefer an existing official or custom package.** Duplicating a domain that already exists is the most common failure here.\n2. **Create a custom package** when nothing provides what you need — `definePackage`, declared resources, declared capability dependencies, versioned policies, function-free metadata (`docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`).\n3. **Never patch the kernel.** If you need something the runtime lacks, that is a missing *generic* runtime capability: raise it as an ADR discussion, do not reach into `packages/core/src`.\n4. A record that must gain a field or a status **evolves** — bump its manifest `revision` (ADR-019, `docs/MODULE_EVOLUTION.md`). Do not model a second parallel record to work around the schema.\n5. A decision that varies by business is a **versioned, fingerprinted policy**, not a hardcoded branch.\n\n## 5. Produce a Solution Plan before writing code\n\nThe plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings — `solutionPlanContract: 1`. The validator below is the contract; `docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md` and the canonical example `examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json` are background where the project carries them.\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- solution validate <plan.json>   # the contract alone; reads no project\nnpm run crm -- solution check    <plan.json>   # validate, then bind to this project's app inspect report\n```\n\nWrite the plan, record the `app inspect` report you read in step 2 into `application`, and run `check` before writing any code. Fix every problem it reports. Re-run it before the review: a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`, and a stale plan is not a plan.\n\nWhat the contract makes non-negotiable, and why each one is there:\n\n- **six decision types**, one per rung of the hierarchy below — `configure`, `extend`, `evolve`, `provider`, `create-package`, `propose-kernel-capability`. Say which rungs you tried in `rungsTried`.\n- **`propose-kernel-capability` may never appear in `steps[]`.** Rung 5 is a proposal you write. Putting it in steps is refused (`PLAN_DECISION_NOT_A_STEP`) because patching the kernel to make a solution fit is exactly what the hierarchy exists to prevent.\n- **six evidence categories and no others**, and every derived metric, inference and recommendation must cite what it follows from. A missing category is a problem too — an omitted gap is a claim.\n- **approval codes are a closed set.** A `provider` step must carry `install_or_configure_provider`; the validator adds the requirement rather than trusting you to remember it.\n- **citations point one way.** A fact and an assumption cite nothing; a derived metric cites facts and assumptions; an inference adds derived metrics; a recommendation adds inferences. Unavailable evidence is never a source. The graph is acyclic by construction, so you cannot cite a conclusion as a premise even by accident.\n- **rung 3 and above must show their work.** `provider`, `create-package` and `propose-kernel-capability` each require every lower rung in `rungsTried`, a reason per rung in `rejectedRungs`, and the capability `gap`. Skipping the inspection is how a domain that already exists gets duplicated.\n- **the composition fingerprint is derived, not written.** Record the `inspectionFingerprint` that `solution check --json` reports. It is a drift detector, not proof of anything — but it is not a label you compose either, and a free-text value is refused.\n- **unknown keys are refused.** If the contract has no field for what you want to say, say it in the fields that exist rather than inventing one.\n- **a plan carries no command.** A step names a decision and the seam it uses. Anything that looks like something to run — a shell command, a URL to fetch, a substitution, a script tag — is refused (`PLAN_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`). That filter is defense in depth; the real boundary is that the shape has no field anything reads as an instruction. Nothing executes a plan, here or anywhere in this framework.\n\nThe plan is what the human reviews. Code that arrives before the plan cannot be reviewed as a solution — only as a diff.\n\n## 6. Distinguish implemented from planned, every time\n\nState clearly which parts of the solution exist, which are in an open PR, and which are roadmap only. **Never present Marketing, Analytics Studio, attribution, durable automation or Cloud capabilities as available.** If the goal needs one of them, say what can be built now, what cannot, and what the missing piece blocks.\n\n## 7. Define the user's experience, not just the data\n\nWhich Admin views, what each shows, what a reader can conclude from it, and where the limitations are stated **on the screen** — a data-quality panel next to a conversion rate is part of the deliverable.\n\n## 8. Define acceptance before building\n\nQuality Gates · `crm package validate` · unit, integration and E2E tests · a clean-clone run · the browser smoke where available · and the exact JTBD row the solution claims to move, with the evidence that would justify it. A row moves only with linked evidence; \"the data model exists\" is not \"the job is done\".\n\n## 9. Ask for approval only at sensitive boundaries\n\nProceed without asking for analysis, proposals, generation, previews and tests. Require a human for: publishing to production · sending any external communication · activating a journey · changing a live audience · using sensitive data · launching ads · creating or increasing spend · installing or configuring a provider · changing secrets · auto-applying an experiment winner · anything irreversible or destructive.\n\nReal approval **roles** need the Production Spine. Until then this is a human-actor boundary, not RBAC — never describe it as secure.\n\n## The decision hierarchy — try each rung before the next\n\n```text\n1. configure an existing package        a policy version, a config value, a view\n2. extend through a declared seam       an action, a policy, a record on a package that owns the domain\n3. add or configure a provider          when the gap is an integration, not a model\n4. create a custom package              when no installed package owns the domain\n5. propose a kernel capability          ONLY with generic, multi-domain evidence, as an ADR discussion\n```\n\nSay which rungs you tried. Rung 5 is a proposal you write, never a step you take inside a solution.\n\n## Evidence-first output, in this order\n\n```text\nObserved facts        what the data says, with the query and its version\nDerived metrics       what was computed, and how\nAssumptions           what was taken as true without evidence\nInferences            what follows from facts plus assumptions\nRecommendations       what to do, traceable to the rows above\nUnavailable evidence  what could not be checked, and why\n```\n\nNo attribution model is causal truth. Where a valid control group exists, report **lift** as the better answer than attribution.\n\n## After the build\n\n```text\nobserve → diagnose → recommend → propose next version\n```\n\n**No silent operational change.** A recommendation is a proposal; applying it is a new plan, a new approval where required, and a new version.\n\n## 10. Finish with evidence, not code\n\nReport: what was built, how it works, the assumptions, the limitations, the Admin views, the tests and their results, the JTBD rows moved and why, and the approvals still outstanding. Run the adversarial review (`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §5) before any merge.\n\nFinish with `npm run verify` and the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`).\n\n## Never\n\nInvent a command, a provider, a package or a capability that does not exist · claim an unimplemented capability is available · claim a proposed MCP tool surface exists · move a JTBD row without linked evidence · patch the kernel to make a solution fit · refactor a legacy domain out of `packages/core` on the way past · send, publish or spend without an explicit human approval · describe any of this as sandboxed or authenticated.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: Put a partner-management area onto disk in this repository. The user wants a partner-management capability built as part of the CRM/Accordo application — discover what already exists (e.g. any partner engagement support inside delivery handover or elsewhere), determine the gap, and build the appropriate package(s) with checked-in source, verified per the project's standard rails."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"42119ee6-c67e-47a0-b778-ae7b902d675c","timestamp":"2026-08-18T07:10:03.001Z","isSynthetic":true}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015QVkYdiRkSDmqSLpw1iYqK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Delivery handover (Milestone 13, the delivery domain package)\n2\t\n3\tTurning the pending Delivery Obligations of an activated contract into a\n4\t**planned** delivery project: one work package per obligation, a milestone\n5\tplan, and an optional third-party partner engagement.\n6\t\n7\t**Milestone 13 plans and records a handover. It does not execute delivery**:\n8\tnothing there starts, progresses or completes, and a Delivery Project is\n9\tcreated `pending_kickoff`. **Milestone 14a** — merged — gives it that\n10\tlifecycle: eight human-driven transitions over an explicit\n11\ttable. Even then, nothing schedules, staffs, costs, bills, accepts or grants\n12\tanyone access, and nothing moves on a clock.\n13\t\n14\tIt is also the **second** domain package, and the first one that depends on\n15\tanother: `packages/delivery` reaches `packages/contracts` only through the\n16\tdeclared capability `delivery-obligations@1`. Neither package imports the\n17\tother's source, and the kernel imports neither.\n18\t\n19\t## The flow\n20\t\n21\t```text\n22\tsigned Order (M11)\n23\t→ activated Contract + pending Delivery Obligations (M12)\n24\t→ plan-delivery-handover     read-only: who delivers what, and why\n25\t→ decide anything the policy would not guess (human, with a reason)\n26\t→ create-delivery-handover   ONE transaction: handover run + delivery project\n27\t                             + work packages + milestones + optional partner\n28\t                             + the obligations marked handed over\n29\t→ inspect                    project / work packages / milestones / partner / audit / trace\n30\t```\n31\t\n32\t```js\n33\t// read-only; safe for an agent to prepare\n34\tconst { plan } = (await client.module('commercial-contract')\n35\t  .action(contractId, 'plan-delivery-handover', { policy: 'b2b-delivery-handover', policyVersion: 1 })).result;\n36\t\n37\t// the human decision (an agent actor is refused 403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED)\n38\tawait client.module('commercial-contract').action(contractId, 'create-delivery-handover', {\n39\t  policy: 'b2b-delivery-handover',\n40\t  policyVersion: 1,\n41\t  projectName: 'Acme — onboarding and migration',\n42\t  targetStartDate: '2026-09-20',      // planning data, not a commitment\n43\t  targetEndDate: '2026-12-20',\n44\t  partner: { partnerRef: 'partner:abc-consulting', partnerName: 'ABC Consulting', reason: 'they run our migrations' },\n45\t  modeOverrides: [{ obligationId, deliveryMode: 'internal', reason: 'our own team loads this data' }],\n46\t});\n47\t```\n48\t\n49\t## Who delivers it is a decision, not an inference\n50\t\n51\tA component's label does not say who performs the work, and the framework never\n52\tguesses from one. The Delivery Handover Policy decides from identity the\n53\tobligation already carries — component key, SKU, product, provider — and\n54\treturns `ambiguous` when it cannot:\n55\t\n56\t| Mode | Meaning |\n57\t|---|---|\n58\t| `internal` | our own team performs this work package |\n59\t| `partner` | a named third party performs it; the project needs a partner engagement |\n60\t| `ambiguous` | **blocks the handover** until a human decides, with a reason |\n61\t\n62\tThe policy also proposes the work-package label and which milestones the\n63\tproject needs (`kickoff`, `delivery`, `go-live` in the starter). Those are\n64\tplanning proposals: nobody signed them.\n65\t\n66\tA human override records both answers — `policyDeliveryMode` (what the policy\n67\tsaid), `deliveryMode` (what applies), `modeOverrideReason` and `overriddenBy` —\n68\tso the handover explains itself years later.\n69\t\n70\t## The partner is a reference, not an account\n71\t\n72\tM13 models **at most one** delivery partner per project, as a business\n73\treference and a name snapshot:\n74\t\n75\t- it grants **no** account, login, portal, invitation, permission or role;\n76\t- there is **no** fee, revenue share, invoice, SLA or acceptance;\n77\t- nothing notifies the partner, and nothing can be sent to them from here.\n78\t\n79\tA partner is required exactly when some work package is partner-delivered\n80\t(`409 PARTNER_REQUIRED`), and refused when none is (`409 PARTNER_NOT_APPLICABLE`).\n81\tMultiple partners per project are deliberately not modelled: a project that\n82\tneeds two is a later milestone, not an unreviewed array.\n83\t\n84\t## Planning dates\n85\t\n86\tCalendar dates, `YYYY-MM-DD`, round-trip validated. Both window dates are\n87\tsupplied together or not at all — \"starts 2026-09-01, ends when it ends\" is not\n88\ta plan anyone can read. The window must sit inside the contract's operational\n89\tterm, and the whole window is optional: a handover can be planned before the\n90\tschedule is known.\n91\t\n92\tEvery record carries `datesSource: \"post-sale-delivery-planning\"`. These dates\n93\twere not signed, they are not a customer commitment, and **nothing fires on\n94\tthem** — there is no scheduler in this framework.\n95\t\n96\t## Plan versus create\n97\t\n98\t| | `plan-delivery-handover` | `create-delivery-handover` |\n99\t|---|---|---|\n100\t| Writes | nothing — no delivery record, no business audit, no event | everything, in one transaction |\n101\t| Actor | any, including agents | `user` only; an agent is `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` |\n102\t| Undecided mode | reported per obligation | refused (`409 DELIVERY_MODE_AMBIGUOUS`) |\n103\t| Trust in the caller | — | none: the plan is recomputed inside the transaction |\n104\t\n105\t## Records\n106\t\n107\tAll five are read-only publicly (`get`/`list`) and exist only through the\n108\thandover action.\n109\t\n110\t| Module | Notes |\n111\t|---|---|\n112\t| `delivery-handover-run` | the audited run: actor, time, source contract and activation, policy version and fingerprint, every decision and override, and the records it created |\n113\t| `delivery-project` | one per contract, `delivery-project:contract:<contractId>`, `pending_kickoff`, with its own customer snapshot |\n114\t| `delivery-work-package` | one per source obligation, with the commercial snapshot and the delivery-mode evidence |\n115\t| `delivery-milestone` | the canonical milestone plan, ordered, with the work packages it covers |\n116\t| `delivery-partner-engagement` | at most one, `planned`, naming the partner and the work packages it covers |\n117\t\n118\t## The cross-package boundary\n119\t\n120\t```text\n121\tdelivery  ──requires──▶  contracts/delivery-obligations@1\n122\t   │                              │\n123\t   └── never imports contracts source, never reads its tables\n124\t```\n125\t\n126\tThe capability offers exactly three methods — read the contract's public facts,\n127\tlist its pending obligations, mark selected ones handed over — created with the\n128\t**caller's** runtime handles so the obligation update commits inside the\n129\tdelivery transaction. A package that did not declare the requirement is refused\n130\teven though the capability exists.\n131\t\n132\tFailure is atomic across the boundary: inject a failure after any write,\n133\tincluding the obligation update, and no delivery record survives while every\n134\tobligation stays `pending_handover`. The retry produces exactly one complete\n135\thandover.\n136\t\n137\t## Running the project (Milestone 14a)\n138\t\n139\tEight actions move a delivery project through execution. Full description in\n140\t[`packages/delivery/README.md`](../packages/delivery/README.md); the rules that\n141\tmatter here:\n142\t\n143\t```text\n144\tdelivery-project        pending_kickoff → in_progress → completed\n145\tdelivery-work-package   planned → in_progress ⇄ blocked, in_progress → completed\n146\tdelivery-milestone      planned → in_progress → completed\n147\t```\n148\t\n149\tEvery declared state is reachable by a shipped action and every declared edge\n150\tis walked by one — checked from the shipped action list, not asserted in prose.\n151\tEvery transition requires `actor.type === 'user'` (a human-actor boundary, not\n152\tDelivery Manager RBAC), a stale `expectedState` is a `409` rather than a silent\n153\toverwrite, work moves only under a running project, and a project closes only\n154\tonce every work package and milestone is completed. Blocking requires a stated\n155\treason and records who and when; resuming clears those fields and the block\n156\tstays in the audit log. `completed` is terminal — there is no reopen.\n157\t\n158\tThe three record manifests reach revision 2 through the generic module\n159\tevolution path (ADR-019 and its addendum 1). No Delivery code entered the\n160\tkernel to make that possible.\n161\t\n162\t## Not modeled — stated, not implied\n163\t\n164\tTime tracking, expenses, cost, margin, economics, resource scheduling,\n165\tcapacity, change requests, deliverables, customer acceptance, billing\n166\tmilestones, invoicing, partner access, partner portal, revenue share, service\n167\tcontracts, entitlements, SLA, support cases, reopening completed work, and any\n168\tscheduler.\n169\t\n170\tThe Admin says so on the screen, the schema says so in `notModeled`, and the\n171\tplan payload carries its own list.\n172\t\n173\t## Evidence\n174\t\n175\t`tests/delivery-handover-e2e.test.js`, `tests/delivery-execution-e2e.test.js`,\n176\t`tests/admin-delivery.test.js`, `tests/package-contract.test.js`,\n177\t`packages/delivery/README.md`,\n178\t`examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`,\n179\t`docs/plans/milestone-13-delivery-handover.md`,\n180\t`docs/plans/milestone-14-delivery-economics-acceptance.md`. Neither package imports the\nother's source, and the kernel imports neither.\n\n## The flow\n\n```text\nsigned Order (M11)\n→ activated Contract + pending Delivery Obligations (M12)\n→ plan-delivery-handover     read-only: who delivers what, and why\n→ decide anything the policy would not guess (human, with a reason)\n→ create-delivery-handover   ONE transaction: handover run + delivery project\n                             + work packages + milestones + optional partner\n                             + the obligations marked handed over\n→ inspect                    project / work packages / milestones / partner / audit / trace\n```\n\n```js\n// read-only; safe for an agent to prepare\nconst { plan } = (await client.module('commercial-contract')\n  .action(contractId, 'plan-delivery-handover', { policy: 'b2b-delivery-handover', policyVersion: 1 })).result;\n\n// the human decision (an agent actor is refused 403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED)\nawait client.module('commercial-contract').action(contractId, 'create-delivery-handover', {\n  policy: 'b2b-delivery-handover',\n  policyVersion: 1,\n  projectName: 'Acme — onboarding and migration',\n  targetStartDate: '2026-09-20',      // planning data, not a commitment\n  targetEndDate: '2026-12-20',\n  partner: { partnerRef: 'partner:abc-consulting', partnerName: 'ABC Consulting', reason: 'they run our migrations' },\n  modeOverrides: [{ obligationId, deliveryMode: 'internal', reason: 'our own team loads this data' }],\n});\n```\n\n## Who delivers it is a decision, not an inference\n\nA component's label does not say who performs the work, and the framework never\nguesses from one. The Delivery Handover Policy decides from identity the\nobligation already carries — component key, SKU, product, provider — and\nreturns `ambiguous` when it cannot:\n\n| Mode | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `internal` | our own team performs this work package |\n| `partner` | a named third party performs it; the project needs a partner engagement |\n| `ambiguous` | **blocks the handover** until a human decides, with a reason |\n\nThe policy also proposes the work-package label and which milestones the\nproject needs (`kickoff`, `delivery`, `go-live` in the starter). Those are\nplanning proposals: nobody signed them.\n\nA human override records both answers — `policyDeliveryMode` (what the policy\nsaid), `deliveryMode` (what applies), `modeOverrideReason` and `overriddenBy` —\nso the handover explains itself years later.\n\n## The partner is a reference, not an account\n\nM13 models **at most one** delivery partner per project, as a business\nreference and a name snapshot:\n\n- it grants **no** account, login, portal, invitation, permission or role;\n- there is **no** fee, revenue share, invoice, SLA or acceptance;\n- nothing notifies the partner, and nothing can be sent to them from here.\n\nA partner is required exactly when some work package is partner-delivered\n(`409 PARTNER_REQUIRED`), and refused when none is (`409 PARTNER_NOT_APPLICABLE`).\nMultiple partners per project are deliberately not modelled: a project that\nneeds two is a later milestone, not an unreviewed array.\n\n## Planning dates\n\nCalendar dates, `YYYY-MM-DD`, round-trip validated. Both window dates are\nsupplied together or not at all — \"starts 2026-09-01, ends when it ends\" is not\na plan anyone can read. The window must sit inside the contract's operational\nterm, and the whole window is optional: a handover can be planned before the\nschedule is known.\n\nEvery record carries `datesSource: \"post-sale-delivery-planning\"`. These dates\nwere not signed, they are not a customer commitment, and **nothing fires on\nthem** — there is no scheduler in this framework.\n\n## Plan versus create\n\n| | `plan-delivery-handover` | `create-delivery-handover` |\n|---|---|---|\n| Writes | nothing — no delivery record, no business audit, no event | everything, in one transaction |\n| Actor | any, including agents | `user` only; an agent is `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` |\n| Undecided mode | reported per obligation | refused (`409 DELIVERY_MODE_AMBIGUOUS`) |\n| Trust in the caller | — | none: the plan is recomputed inside the transaction |\n\n## Records\n\nAll five are read-only publicly (`get`/`list`) and exist only through the\nhandover action.\n\n| Module | Notes |\n|---|---|\n| `delivery-handover-run` | the audited run: actor, time, source contract and activation, policy version and fingerprint, every decision and override, and the records it created |\n| `delivery-project` | one per contract, `delivery-project:contract:<contractId>`, `pending_kickoff`, with its own customer snapshot |\n| `delivery-work-package` | one per source obligation, with the commercial snapshot and the delivery-mode evidence |\n| `delivery-milestone` | the canonical milestone plan, ordered, with the work packages it covers |\n| `delivery-partner-engagement` | at most one, `planned`, naming the partner and the work packages it covers |\n\n## The cross-package boundary\n\n```text\ndelivery  ──requires──▶  contracts/delivery-obligations@1\n   │                              │\n   └── never imports contracts source, never reads its tables\n```\n\nThe capability offers exactly three methods — read the contract's public facts,\nlist its pending obligations, mark selected ones handed over — created with the\n**caller's** runtime handles so the obligation update commits inside the\ndelivery transaction. A package that did not declare the requirement is refused\neven though the capability exists.\n\nFailure is atomic across the boundary: inject a failure after any write,\nincluding the obligation update, and no delivery record survives while every\nobligation stays `pending_handover`. The retry produces exactly one complete\nhandover.\n\n## Running the project (Milestone 14a)\n\nEight actions move a delivery project through execution. Full description in\n[`packages/delivery/README.md`](../packages/delivery/README.md); the rules that\nmatter here:\n\n```text\ndelivery-project        pending_kickoff → in_progress → completed\ndelivery-work-package   planned → in_progress ⇄ blocked, in_progress → completed\ndelivery-milestone      planned → in_progress → completed\n```\n\nEvery declared state is reachable by a shipped action and every declared edge\nis walked by one — checked from the shipped action list, not asserted in prose.\nEvery transition requires `actor.type === 'user'` (a human-actor boundary, not\nDelivery Manager RBAC), a stale `expectedState` is a `409` rather than a silent\noverwrite, work moves only under a running project, and a project closes only\nonce every work package and milestone is completed. Blocking requires a stated\nreason and records who and when; resuming clears those fields and the block\nstays in the audit log. `completed` is terminal — there is no reopen.\n\nThe three record manifests reach revision 2 through the generic module\nevolution path (ADR-019 and its addendum 1). No Delivery code entered the\nkernel to make that possible.\n\n## Not modeled — stated, not implied\n\nTime tracking, expenses, cost, margin, economics, resource scheduling,\ncapacity, change requests, deliverables, customer acceptance, billing\nmilestones, invoicing, partner access, partner portal, revenue share, service\ncontracts, entitlements, SLA, support cases, reopening completed work, and any\nscheduler.\n\nThe Admin says so on the screen, the schema says so in `notModeled`, and the\nplan payload carries its own list.\n\n## Evidence\n\n`tests/delivery-handover-e2e.test.js`, `tests/delivery-execution-e2e.test.js`,\n`tests/admin-delivery.test.js`, `tests/package-contract.test.js`,\n`packages/delivery/README.md`,\n`examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`,\n`docs/plans/milestone-13-delivery-handover.md`,\n`docs/plans/milestone-14-delivery-economics-acceptance.md`. Agent instructions:\n`.claude/skills/build-delivery-handover/SKILL.md` (this file is the\nCodex-readable mirror).\n","numLines":183,"startLine":1,"totalLines":183}}}

A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Selecting an Accordo rail\n27\t\n28\tThe application answers questions about itself through a small set of rails —\n29\tfew tools, distinct jobs. When one of the questions below is the question in\n30\tfront of you, run its rail rather than assembling the answer from `find`,\n31\t`grep` and source reading: hand-assembly is slower, and it tends to fail by\n32\tinventing a capability the application does not have\n33\t(`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`).\n34\t\n35\t| The question in front of you | Rail | Command |\n36\t|---|---|---|\n37\t| What has this project actually composed? | SEE | `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` |\n38\t| Is this plan valid, and still true of this application? | PLAN | `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` |\n39\t| What is inconsistent or stale in this checkout? | CHECK | `npm run crm -- project doctor --json` |\n40\t| I need a minimal new package starting point. | BUILD | `npm run crm -- package scaffold <name>` |\n41\t| Does this one package conform to the framework? | CHECK | `npm run crm -- package test <dir> --json` |\n42\t| Is the whole project technically healthy? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- project verify --json` |\n43\t| Does this business journey actually work? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- scenario run <scenario> --json` |\n44\t| Is every requirement of the plan actually proven? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- solution verify <plan.json> --evidence <evidence.json>` |\n45\t| Will this refactor preserve behaviour? | PRESERVE | characterization — `tests/characterization/`, `npm run characterize:intelligence` |\n46\t\n47\t**The selection rule.** Use the smallest rail that directly answers the current\n48\tquestion. Do not chain rails automatically, and no rail — `app inspect`\n49\tincluded — is an obligatory first step. Escalate only when the next question\n50\tneeds a stronger kind of evidence: what exists → inspect · internally\n51\tconsistent → doctor · technical health proven → project verify · this journey\n52\tproven → scenario run · the plan proven complete → solution verify.\n53\t\n54\tThe boundaries between neighbouring rails:\n55\t\n56\t- `app inspect` describes what exists — composition, capabilities, records,\n57\t  actions, policies, providers — never health, domain correctness or runtime state.\n58\t- `project doctor` diagnoses source consistency and drift; it makes no claim\n59\t  about business behaviour.\n60\t- `solution check` asks whether the plan still matches the application\n61\t  (`PLAN_STALE`), not whether it is implemented: it can exit 0 on a plan\n62\t  nobody has built a line of.\n63\t- `project verify` proves technical health by orchestrating existing authorities\n64\t  — conformance, the doctor, the project's declared scripts; it runs no business\n65\t  scenario and maps no requirement to proof.\n66\t- `scenario run` proves one named business journey with linked evidence; it is\n67\t  not whole-project health, and it promotes no JTBD row.\n68\t- `solution verify` maps every plan requirement to machine-checked proof and\n69\t  may honestly exit 1 with work unproven; it executes no plan and writes\n70\t  nothing.\n71\t- `package scaffold` writes a minimal conforming skeleton with no invented\n72\t  domain semantics — dry-run by default, `--apply` to write.\n73\t- `package test` proves framework conformance by composing the package into a\n74\t  real application, never domain correctness; `package validate` checks only\n75\t  that the declaration is structurally valid, so a conformance question ends\n76\t  at `test`.\n77\t- characterization freezes a domain's externally observable behaviour before a\n78\t  boundary-preserving refactor, and replays it after.\n79\t\n80\tSEE, PLAN, BUILD, CHECK, PROVE and PRESERVE are the agent's internal labels\n81\tfor its own next action. A user states a goal and never needs to know a rail\n82\texists; for goal-shaped work, the Skills choose the rails.\n83\t\n84\tThis section appears verbatim in both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, because\n85\teach is the file a different harness loads at session start. If the copies\n86\tdisagree, `AGENTS.md` is canonical. Parity is kept by hand — diff the two\n87\tsections when editing either.\n88\t\n89\t## Parallel coding agents\n90\t\n91\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n92\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n93\t\n94\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n95\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n96\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n97\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n98\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n99\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n100\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n101\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n102\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n103\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n104\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n105\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n106\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n107\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n108\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n109\t\n110\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n111\t\n112\t## Coding conventions\n113\t\n114\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n115\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n116\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n117\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n118\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n119\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n120\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n121\t\n122\t## Definition of done\n123\t\n124\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n125\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n126\t- trace and audit are visible;\n127\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n128\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n129\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n130\t\n131\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n132\t\n133\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n134\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n135\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n136\t\n137\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n138\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n139\t> reliability or evidence.\n140\t\n141\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n142\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n143\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n144\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n145\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n146\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n147\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n148\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n149\t\n150\t## Code review rules\n151\t\n152\t- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n153\t- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n154\t- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n155\t- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n156\t- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n157\t- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n158\t- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n\n## Selecting an Accordo rail\n\nThe application answers questions about itself through a small set of rails —\nfew tools, distinct jobs. When one of the questions below is the question in\nfront of you, run its rail rather than assembling the answer from `find`,\n`grep` and source reading: hand-assembly is slower, and it tends to fail by\ninventing a capability the application does not have\n(`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`).\n\n| The question in front of you | Rail | Command |\n|---|---|---|\n| What has this project actually composed? | SEE | `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` |\n| Is this plan valid, and still true of this application? | PLAN | `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` |\n| What is inconsistent or stale in this checkout? | CHECK | `npm run crm -- project doctor --json` |\n| I need a minimal new package starting point. | BUILD | `npm run crm -- package scaffold <name>` |\n| Does this one package conform to the framework? | CHECK | `npm run crm -- package test <dir> --json` |\n| Is the whole project technically healthy? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- project verify --json` |\n| Does this business journey actually work? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- scenario run <scenario> --json` |\n| Is every requirement of the plan actually proven? | PROVE | `npm run crm -- solution verify <plan.json> --evidence <evidence.json>` |\n| Will this refactor preserve behaviour? | PRESERVE | characterization — `tests/characterization/`, `npm run characterize:intelligence` |\n\n**The selection rule.** Use the smallest rail that directly answers the current\nquestion. Do not chain rails automatically, and no rail — `app inspect`\nincluded — is an obligatory first step. Escalate only when the next question\nneeds a stronger kind of evidence: what exists → inspect · internally\nconsistent → doctor · technical health proven → project verify · this journey\nproven → scenario run · the plan proven complete → solution verify.\n\nThe boundaries between neighbouring rails:\n\n- `app inspect` describes what exists — composition, capabilities, records,\n  actions, policies, providers — never health, domain correctness or runtime state.\n- `project doctor` diagnoses source consistency and drift; it makes no claim\n  about business behaviour.\n- `solution check` asks whether the plan still matches the application\n  (`PLAN_STALE`), not whether it is implemented: it can exit 0 on a plan\n  nobody has built a line of.\n- `project verify` proves technical health by orchestrating existing authorities\n  — conformance, the doctor, the project's declared scripts; it runs no business\n  scenario and maps no requirement to proof.\n- `scenario run` proves one named business journey with linked evidence; it is\n  not whole-project health, and it promotes no JTBD row.\n- `solution verify` maps every plan requirement to machine-checked proof and\n  may honestly exit 1 with work unproven; it executes no plan and writes\n  nothing.\n- `package scaffold` writes a minimal conforming skeleton with no invented\n  domain semantics — dry-run by default, `--apply` to write.\n- `package test` proves framework conformance by composing the package into a\n  real application, never domain correctness; `package validate` checks only\n  that the declaration is structurally valid, so a conformance question ends\n  at `test`.\n- characterization freezes a domain's externally observable behaviour before a\n  boundary-preserving refactor, and replays it after.\n\nSEE, PLAN, BUILD, CHECK, PROVE and PRESERVE are the agent's internal labels\nfor its own next action. A user states a goal and never needs to know a rail\nexists; for goal-shaped work, the Skills choose the rails.\n\nThis section appears verbatim in both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, because\neach is the file a different harness loads at session start. If the copies\ndisagree, `AGENTS.md` is canonical. Parity is kept by hand — diff the two\nsections when editing either.\n\n## Parallel coding agents\n\nSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\nany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n\n1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n   lands in another agent's tree.\n2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n\nSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n\n## Coding conventions\n\n- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- behavior works end-to-end;\n- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n- trace and audit are visible;\n- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n\n## The DX Simplicity Gate\n\nBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\nanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n\n> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n> reliability or evidence.\n\n- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n\n## Code review rules\n\n- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n","numLines":159,"startLine":1,"totalLines":159}}}
